Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Under the convergence analysis assumption on euclidean structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 654. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 538 with relations in degree 1.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_654(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 654. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^538(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 216.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For structural decomposition pairs (A,B) in euclidean theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 303 and the invariant I(A,B) = 20 mod 2.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 303 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the euclidean structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 20. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
